        Idea:
Collapse the three parallel resize session kinds in the video-editor ('clip' / 'group-child-boundary' / 'group-edge') into ONE unified ClipEdgeResizeSession with a single applyClipEdgeMove function that dispatches on context internally. This is the missing structural item from the previous cleanup megaplan — that one extracted shared math functions but left the session lifecycle (setup → update → end → commit) triplicated, and the triplication is the cause of 'I can't even tell which path the user's gesture went through' during debugging.

State BEFORE this megaplan:

DONE:
- One ResizeSession discriminated union with three kinds: 'clip', 'group-child-boundary', 'group-edge'
- Shared pure math functions in src/tools/video-editor/lib/resize-math.ts: computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, computeGroupEdgeResize
- cursorOffsetPx captured at pointer-down for all three kinds
- Single ref-backed preview store via useSyncExternalStore
- useTimelineScale hook centralizing pixel↔time
- 12 end-to-end resize integration tests in TimelineCanvas.test.tsx covering off-center cursor on interior boundary and group-edge drags
- 222/222 video-editor tests passing
- tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for video-editor
- A temporary [resize-diag] console.log in the 'group-child-boundary' branch of updateResize (needs removing)

NOT DONE (this megaplan):
- The session lifecycle is still TRIPLICATED: three branches each in handleResizePointerDown, updateResize, endResize
- 'group-edge' uses a special startGroupEdgeResize closure pattern that captures commitResize at session start
- useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd has its own internal branching for 'interior vs free' via getInteriorNeighbor

Target shape:

ONE session type: ClipEdgeResizeSession with fields { pointerId, rowId, clipId, edge: 'left'|'right', cursorOffsetPx, initialBoundaryTime, context: ClipEdgeResizeContext }. The discriminator moves from session to context sub-object.

ClipEdgeResizeContext =
 | { kind: 'free'; initialStart; initialEnd; minStart?; maxEnd? }
 | { kind: 'interior'; pairStart; pairEnd; draggedClipId; adjacentClipId; draggedInitialStart; draggedInitialEnd; adjacentInitialStart; adjacentInitialEnd }
 | { kind: 'outer'; shotId; trackId; groupInitialStart; groupInitialEnd; groupClipIds; groupChildrenSnapshot: Array<{clipId;start;end}> }

ONE core function in lib/resize-math.ts:
applyClipEdgeMove(context, edge, newBoundaryTime) -> { updates: Array<{clipId, start, end}>, wasClamped: boolean }
Dispatches internally on context.kind. 'free' returns one update, 'interior' calls computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, 'outer' calls computeGroupEdgeResize and projects children.

ONE updateResize: computes newBoundaryTime from clientX - cursorOffsetPx, calls applyClipEdgeMove, writes updates to override store. Zero branches on kind.

ONE endResize: one branch on cancelled, no branch on session kind. Calls applyClipEdgeMove one more time and invokes onClipEdgeResizeEnd with updates.

ONE commit path in useClipResize.ts: onClipEdgeResizeEnd takes { session, updates } and builds one applyEdit. startGroupEdgeResize closure is REMOVED.

ONE handleResizePointerDown: uses new resolveClipEdgeResizeContext helper to resolve context up-front based on rows/shotGroups/positionedShotGroups/clipId/rowId/edge, then constructs the unified session.

Hard requirements:
1. All 222 existing video-editor tests pass (some may be rewritten to test by context)
2. The 12 end-to-end resize integration tests survive unchanged
3. Add at least one new end-to-end test for free-clip resize (the 'free' context)
4. tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for src/tools/video-editor/
5. The three session kinds are physically gone: grep for kind: 'group-child-boundary' and kind: 'group-edge' in resize lifecycle code returns zero. Three update branches in updateResize and three end branches in endResize are gone. startGroupEdgeResize closure removed.
6. applyClipEdgeMove exists in lib/resize-math.ts with unit tests covering all three contexts
7. The [resize-diag] console.log is removed
8. No regressions in: free clip resize, interior boundary drag, outer edge drag, body drag inside pinned group (useClipDrag - should be untouched), cross-track grouped drag, switch to/from final video, auto-save deferral, migration load, cursor offset tracking

Constraints:
- Scope: resize lifecycle only
- Touch: TimelineCanvas.tsx, useClipResize.ts, lib/resize-math.ts, lib/resize-math.test.ts, TimelineCanvas.test.tsx, possibly TimelineEditorContext.tsx/useTimelineState.types.ts (if startGroupEdgeResize exposed)
- Do NOT touch useClipDrag.ts, data shape, save/sync/persistence, projection migration
- Use existing computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, computeGroupEdgeResize, computeClipResizeMetaPatch inside the new dispatcher — don't reimplement math
- Prefer Edit over Write. Follow CLAUDE.md
- Leave pre-existing non-video-editor test/tsc issues alone

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

const distance = Math.abs(boundaryTime - siblingTime);\n+    if (distance < closestDistance) {\n+      closestDistance = distance;\n+      snappedBoundary = siblingTime;\n+    }\n+  }\n+\n+  return snappedBoundary;\n+}\n+\n+const applyFreeClipEdgeMove = (\n+  context: FreeClipEdgeResizeContext,\n+  edge: ResizeDir,\n+  newBoundaryTime: number,\n+): ApplyClipEdgeMoveResult => {\n+  if (edge === resized.end }],\n+      wasClamped: resized.wasClamped,\n+    };\n+  }\n+\n+  const resized = computeGroupEdgeResize({\n+    dir: edge,\n+    initial: { start: context.initialStart, end: context.initialEnd },\n+    proposedBoundary: newBoundaryTime,\n+    minimumDuration: MIN_CLIP_EDGE_RESIZE_DURATION,\n+  });\n+\n+  let end = resized.end;\n+  let wasClamped = resized.wasClamped;\n+ maximumEnd;\n+      wasClamped = true;\n+    }\n+  }\n+\n+  return {\n+    updates: [{ clipId: context.clipId, start: resized.start, end }],\n+    wasClamped,\n+  };\n+};\n+\n+const applyInteriorClipEdgeMove = (\n+  context: InteriorClipEdgeResizeContext,\n+  edge: ResizeDir,\n+  newBoundaryTime: number,\n+): ApplyClipEdgeMoveResult => {\n+  const resized = computeGroupChildBoundaryResize({\n+    dir: edge,\n+    dragged: { start: context.draggedInitialStart, end: context.draggedInitialEnd },\n+    adjacent: { start: context.adjacentInitialStart, end: context.adjacentInitialEnd },\n+    proposedBoundary: newBoundaryTime,\n+    minimumDuration: MIN_CLIP_EDGE_RESIZE_DURATION,\n+  });\n+\n+  return {\n+    updates: [\n+      { clipId: wasClamped: resized.wasClamped,\n+  };\n+};\n+\n+const applyOuterClipEdgeMove = (\n+  context: OuterClipEdgeResizeContext,\n+  edge: ResizeDir,\n+  newBoundaryTime: number,\n+): newBoundaryTime,\n+    minimumDuration: MIN_CLIP_EDGE_RESIZE_DURATION,\n+  });\n+\n+  const originalDuration = context.groupInitialEnd - context.groupInitialStart;\n+  const nextDuration = resized.end - resized.start;\n+  const scale = originalDuration > 0 ? nextDuration / originalDuration : 1;\n+\n+  return {\n+    updates: context.groupChildrenSnapshot.map((child) => {\n+      const offset = child.start - context.groupInitialStart;\n+      const duration = child.end - child.start;\n+      const start = resized.start + offset * scale;\n+      return {\n+        clipId: child.clipId,\n+ > DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Collapse the three parallel resize session kinds in the video-editor ('clip' / 'group-child-boundary' / 'group-edge') into ONE unified ClipEdgeResizeSession with a single applyClipEdgeMove function that dispatches on context internally. This is the missing structural item from the previous cleanup megaplan — that one extracted shared math functions but left the session lifecycle (setup → update → end → commit) triplicated, and the triplication is the cause of 'I can't even tell which path the user's gesture went through' during debugging.

State BEFORE this megaplan:

DONE:
- One ResizeSession discriminated union with three kinds: 'clip', 'group-child-boundary', 'group-edge'
- Shared pure math functions in src/tools/video-editor/lib/resize-math.ts: computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, computeGroupEdgeResize
- cursorOffsetPx captured at pointer-down for all three kinds
- Single ref-backed preview store via useSyncExternalStore
- useTimelineScale hook centralizing pixel↔time
- 12 end-to-end resize integration tests in TimelineCanvas.test.tsx covering off-center cursor on interior boundary and group-edge drags
- 222/222 video-editor tests passing
- tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for video-editor
- A temporary [resize-diag] console.log in the 'group-child-boundary' branch of updateResize (needs removing)

NOT DONE (this megaplan):
- The session lifecycle is still TRIPLICATED: three branches each in handleResizePointerDown, updateResize, endResize
- 'group-edge' uses a special startGroupEdgeResize closure pattern that captures commitResize at session start
- useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd has its own internal branching for 'interior vs free' via getInteriorNeighbor

Target shape:

ONE session type: ClipEdgeResizeSession with fields { pointerId, rowId, clipId, edge: 'left'|'right', cursorOffsetPx, initialBoundaryTime, context: ClipEdgeResizeContext }. The discriminator moves from session to context sub-object.

ClipEdgeResizeContext =
 | { kind: 'free'; initialStart; initialEnd; minStart?; maxEnd? }
 | { kind: 'interior'; pairStart; pairEnd; draggedClipId; adjacentClipId; draggedInitialStart; draggedInitialEnd; adjacentInitialStart; adjacentInitialEnd }
 | { kind: 'outer'; shotId; trackId; groupInitialStart; groupInitialEnd; groupClipIds; groupChildrenSnapshot: Array<{clipId;start;end}> }

ONE core function in lib/resize-math.ts:
applyClipEdgeMove(context, edge, newBoundaryTime) -> { updates: Array<{clipId, start, end}>, wasClamped: boolean }
Dispatches internally on context.kind. 'free' returns one update, 'interior' calls computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, 'outer' calls computeGroupEdgeResize and projects children.

ONE updateResize: computes newBoundaryTime from clientX - cursorOffsetPx, calls applyClipEdgeMove, writes updates to override store. Zero branches on kind.

ONE endResize: one branch on cancelled, no branch on session kind. Calls applyClipEdgeMove one more time and invokes onClipEdgeResizeEnd with updates.

ONE commit path in useClipResize.ts: onClipEdgeResizeEnd takes { session, updates } and builds one applyEdit. startGroupEdgeResize closure is REMOVED.

ONE handleResizePointerDown: uses new resolveClipEdgeResizeContext helper to resolve context up-front based on rows/shotGroups/positionedShotGroups/clipId/rowId/edge, then constructs the unified session.

Hard requirements:
1. All 222 existing video-editor tests pass (some may be rewritten to test by context)
2. The 12 end-to-end resize integration tests survive unchanged
3. Add at least one new end-to-end test for free-clip resize (the 'free' context)
4. tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for src/tools/video-editor/
5. The three session kinds are physically gone: grep for kind: 'group-child-boundary' and kind: 'group-edge' in resize lifecycle code returns zero. Three update branches in updateResize and three end branches in endResize are gone. startGroupEdgeResize closure removed.
6. applyClipEdgeMove exists in lib/resize-math.ts with unit tests covering all three contexts
7. The [resize-diag] console.log is removed
8. No regressions in: free clip resize, interior boundary drag, outer edge drag, body drag inside pinned group (useClipDrag - should be untouched), cross-track grouped drag, switch to/from final video, auto-save deferral, migration load, cursor offset tracking

Constraints:
- Scope: resize lifecycle only
- Touch: TimelineCanvas.tsx, useClipResize.ts, lib/resize-math.ts, lib/resize-math.test.ts, TimelineCanvas.test.tsx, possibly TimelineEditorContext.tsx/useTimelineState.types.ts (if startGroupEdgeResize exposed)
- Do NOT touch useClipDrag.ts, data shape, save/sync/persistence, projection migration
- Use existing computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, computeGroupEdgeResize, computeClipResizeMetaPatch inside the new dispatcher — don't reimplement math
- Prefer Edit over Write. Follow CLAUDE.md
- Leave pre-existing non-video-editor test/tsc issues alone

Success criteria:
1. 'boundary doesn't track cursor during drag' symptom cannot reproduce in unified path
2. tsc clean for src/tools/video-editor/
3. All video-editor tests pass — >=223 after adding free-clip test
4. Grep for kind: 'group-child-boundary' and kind: 'group-edge' returns zero in resize code
5. Grep for startGroupEdgeResize returns zero
6. [resize-diag] console.log gone
7. applyClipEdgeMove and resolveClipEdgeResizeContext exist with unit tests
8. Resize lifecycle code line count DECREASES net

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx\n@@\n const getResizePreviewIds = (session: ClipEdgeResizeSession): string[] => {\n   switch (session.context.kind) {\n@@\n   }\n };\n+\n+const getPreviewUpdatesFromSnapshot = (\n+  session: ClipEdgeResizeSession,\n+  snapshot: Readonly<Record<string, ResizeOverride>>,\n+): ClipEdgeResizeUpdate[] => {\n+  switch (session.context.kind) {\n+    case 'free': {\n+      const override = snapshot[session.clipId];\n+      return [{\n+        clipId: session.clipId,\n+        start: override?.start ?? session.context.initialStart,\n+        end: override?.end ?? session.context.initialEnd,\n+      }];\n+    }\n+    case 'interior': {\n+      const draggedOverride = snapshot[session.clipId];\n+      const adjacentOverride = snapshot[session.context.adjacentClipId];\n+      return [\n+        {\n+          clipId: session.clipId,\n+          start: draggedOverride?.start ?? session.context.draggedInitialStart,\n+          end: draggedOverride?.end ?? session.context.draggedInitialEnd,\n+        },\n+        {\n+          clipId: session.context.adjacentClipId,\n+          start: adjacentOverride?.start ?? session.context.adjacentInitialStart,\n+          end: adjacentOverride?.end ?? session.context.adjacentInitialEnd,\n+        },\n+      ];\n+    }\n+    case 'outer':\n+      return session.context.groupChildrenSnapshot.map((child) => {\n+        const override = snapshot[child.clipId];\n+        return {\n+          clipId: child.clipId,\n+          start: override?.start ?? child.start,\n+          end: override?.end ?? child.end,\n+        };\n+      });\n+  }\n+};\n*** End Patch\n"
}

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Collapse the three parallel resize session kinds in the video-editor ('clip' / 'group-child-boundary' / 'group-edge') into ONE unified ClipEdgeResizeSession with a single applyClipEdgeMove function that dispatches on context internally. This is the missing structural item from the previous cleanup megaplan — that one extracted shared math functions but left the session lifecycle (setup → update → end → commit) triplicated, and the triplication is the cause of 'I can't even tell which path the user's gesture went through' during debugging.

State BEFORE this megaplan:

DONE:
- One ResizeSession discriminated union with three kinds: 'clip', 'group-child-boundary', 'group-edge'
- Shared pure math functions in src/tools/video-editor/lib/resize-math.ts: computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, computeGroupEdgeResize
- cursorOffsetPx captured at pointer-down for all three kinds
- Single ref-backed preview store via useSyncExternalStore
- useTimelineScale hook centralizing pixel↔time
- 12 end-to-end resize integration tests in TimelineCanvas.test.tsx covering off-center cursor on interior boundary and group-edge drags
- 222/222 video-editor tests passing
- tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for video-editor
- A temporary [resize-diag] console.log in the 'group-child-boundary' branch of updateResize (needs removing)

NOT DONE (this megaplan):
- The session lifecycle is still TRIPLICATED: three branches each in handleResizePointerDown, updateResize, endResize
- 'group-edge' uses a special startGroupEdgeResize closure pattern that captures commitResize at session start
- useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd has its own internal branching for 'interior vs free' via getInteriorNeighbor

Target shape:

ONE session type: ClipEdgeResizeSession with fields { pointerId, rowId, clipId, edge: 'left'|'right', cursorOffsetPx, initialBoundaryTime, context: ClipEdgeResizeContext }. The discriminator moves from session to context sub-object.

ClipEdgeResizeContext =
 | { kind: 'free'; initialStart; initialEnd; minStart?; maxEnd? }
 | { kind: 'interior'; pairStart; pairEnd; draggedClipId; adjacentClipId; draggedInitialStart; draggedInitialEnd; adjacentInitialStart; adjacentInitialEnd }
 | { kind: 'outer'; shotId; trackId; groupInitialStart; groupInitialEnd; groupClipIds; groupChildrenSnapshot: Array<{clipId;start;end}> }

ONE core function in lib/resize-math.ts:
applyClipEdgeMove(context, edge, newBoundaryTime) -> { updates: Array<{clipId, start, end}>, wasClamped: boolean }
Dispatches internally on context.kind. 'free' returns one update, 'interior' calls computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, 'outer' calls computeGroupEdgeResize and projects children.

ONE updateResize: computes newBoundaryTime from clientX - cursorOffsetPx, calls applyClipEdgeMove, writes updates to override store. Zero branches on kind.

ONE endResize: one branch on cancelled, no branch on session kind. Calls applyClipEdgeMove one more time and invokes onClipEdgeResizeEnd with updates.

ONE commit path in useClipResize.ts: onClipEdgeResizeEnd takes { session, updates } and builds one applyEdit. startGroupEdgeResize closure is REMOVED.

ONE handleResizePointerDown: uses new resolveClipEdgeResizeContext helper to resolve context up-front based on rows/shotGroups/positionedShotGroups/clipId/rowId/edge, then constructs the unified session.

Hard requirements:
1. All 222 existing video-editor tests pass (some may be rewritten to test by context)
2. The 12 end-to-end resize integration tests survive unchanged
3. Add at least one new end-to-end test for free-clip resize (the 'free' context)
4. tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for src/tools/video-editor/
5. The three session kinds are physically gone: grep for kind: 'group-child-boundary' and kind: 'group-edge' in resize lifecycle code returns zero. Three update branches in updateResize and three end branches in endResize are gone. startGroupEdgeResize closure removed.
6. applyClipEdgeMove exists in lib/resize-math.ts with unit tests covering all three contexts
7. The [resize-diag] console.log is removed
8. No regressions in: free clip resize, interior boundary drag, outer edge drag, body drag inside pinned group (useClipDrag - should be untouched), cross-track grouped drag, switch to/from final video, auto-save deferral, migration load, cursor offset tracking

Constraints:
- Scope: resize lifecycle only
- Touch: TimelineCanvas.tsx, useClipResize.ts, lib/resize-math.ts, lib/resize-math.test.ts, TimelineCanvas.test.tsx, possibly TimelineEditorContext.tsx/useTimelineState.types.ts (if startGroupEdgeResize exposed)
- Do NOT touch useClipDrag.ts, data shape, save/sync/persistence, projection migration
- Use existing computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, computeGroupEdgeResize, computeClipResizeMetaPatch inside the new dispatcher — don't reimplement math
- Prefer Edit over Write. Follow CLAUDE.md
- Leave pre-existing non-video-editor test/tsc issues alone

Success criteria:

2. tsc clean for src/tools/video-editor/
3. All video-editor tests pass — >=223 after adding free-clip test
4. Grep for kind: 'group-child-boundary' and kind: 'group-edge' returns zero in resize code
5. Grep for startGroupEdgeResize returns zero
6. [resize-diag] console.log gone
7. applyClipEdgeMove and resolveClipEdgeResizeContext exist with unit tests

        Idea:
Collapse the three parallel resize session kinds in the video-editor ('clip' / 'group-child-boundary' / 'group-edge') into ONE unified ClipEdgeResizeSession with a single applyClipEdgeMove function that dispatches on context internally. This is the missing structural item from the previous cleanup megaplan — that one extracted shared math functions but left the session lifecycle (setup → update → end → commit) triplicated, and the triplication is the cause of 'I can't even tell which path the user's gesture went through' during debugging.

State BEFORE this megaplan:

DONE:
- One ResizeSession discriminated union with three kinds: 'clip', 'group-child-boundary', 'group-edge'
- Shared pure math functions in src/tools/video-editor/lib/resize-math.ts: computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, computeGroupEdgeResize
- cursorOffsetPx captured at pointer-down for all three kinds
- Single ref-backed preview store via useSyncExternalStore
- useTimelineScale hook centralizing pixel↔time
- 12 end-to-end resize integration tests in TimelineCanvas.test.tsx covering off-center cursor on interior boundary and group-edge drags
- 222/222 video-editor tests passing
- tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for video-editor
- A temporary [resize-diag] console.log in the 'group-child-boundary' branch of updateResize (needs removing)

NOT DONE (this megaplan):
- The session lifecycle is still TRIPLICATED: three branches each in handleResizePointerDown, updateResize, endResize
- 'group-edge' uses a special startGroupEdgeResize closure pattern that captures commitResize at session start
- useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd has its own internal branching for 'interior vs free' via getInteriorNeighbor

Target shape:

ONE session type: ClipEdgeResizeSession with fields { pointerId, rowId, clipId, edge: 'left'|'right', cursorOffsetPx, initialBoundaryTime, context: ClipEdgeResizeContext }. The discriminator moves from session to context sub-object.

ClipEdgeResizeContext =
 | { kind: 'free'; initialStart; initialEnd; minStart?; maxEnd? }
 | { kind: 'interior'; pairStart; pairEnd; draggedClipId; adjacentClipId; draggedInitialStart; draggedInitialEnd; adjacentInitialStart; adjacentInitialEnd }
 | { kind: 'outer'; shotId; trackId; groupInitialStart; groupInitialEnd; groupClipIds; groupChildrenSnapshot: Array<{clipId;start;end}> }

ONE core function in lib/resize-math.ts:
applyClipEdgeMove(context, edge, newBoundaryTime) -> { updates: Array<{clipId, start, end}>, wasClamped: boolean }
Dispatches internally on context.kind. 'free' returns one update, 'interior' calls computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, 'outer' calls computeGroupEdgeResize and projects children.

ONE updateResize: computes newBoundaryTime from clientX - cursorOffsetPx, calls applyClipEdgeMove, writes updates to override store. Zero branches on kind.

ONE endResize: one branch on cancelled, no branch on session kind. Calls applyClipEdgeMove one more time and invokes onClipEdgeResizeEnd with updates.

ONE commit path in useClipResize.ts: onClipEdgeResizeEnd takes { session, updates } and builds one applyEdit. startGroupEdgeResize closure is REMOVED.

ONE handleResizePointerDown: uses new resolveClipEdgeResizeContext helper to resolve context up-front based on rows/shotGroups/positionedShotGroups/clipId/rowId/edge, then constructs the unified session.

Hard requirements:
1. All 222 existing video-editor tests pass (some may be rewritten to test by context)
2. The 12 end-to-end resize integration tests survive unchanged
3. Add at least one new end-to-end test for free-clip resize (the 'free' context)
4. tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for src/tools/video-editor/
5. The three session kinds are physically gone: grep for kind: 'group-child-boundary' and kind: 'group-edge' in resize lifecycle code returns zero. Three update branches in updateResize and three end branches in endResize are gone. startGroupEdgeResize closure removed.
6. applyClipEdgeMove exists in lib/resize-math.ts with unit tests covering all three contexts
7. The [resize-diag] console.log is removed
8. No regressions in: free clip resize, interior boundary drag, outer edge drag, body drag inside pinned group (useClipDrag - should be untouched), cross-track grouped drag, switch to/from final video, auto-save deferral, migration load, cursor offset tracking

Constraints:
- Scope: resize lifecycle only
- Touch: TimelineCanvas.tsx, useClipResize.ts, lib/resize-math.ts, lib/resize-math.test.ts, TimelineCanvas.test.tsx, possibly TimelineEditorContext.tsx/useTimelineState.types.ts (if startGroupEdgeResize exposed)
- Do NOT touch useClipDrag.ts, data shape, save/sync/persistence, projection migration
- Use existing computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, computeGroupEdgeResize, computeClipResizeMetaPatch inside the new dispatcher — don't reimplement math
- Prefer Edit over Write. Follow CLAUDE.md
- Leave pre-existing non-video-editor test/tsc issues alone

Success criteria:
1. 'boundary doesn't track cursor during drag' symptom cannot reproduce in unified path
2. tsc clean for src/tools/video-editor/
3. All video-editor tests pass — >=223 after adding free-clip test
4. Grep for kind: 'group-child-boundary' and kind: 'group-edge' returns zero in resize code
5. Grep for startGroupEdgeResize returns zero
6. [resize-diag] console.log gone
7. applyClipEdgeMove and resolveClipEdgeResizeContext exist with unit tests
8. Resize lifecycle code line count DECREASES net

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Collapse the three parallel resize session kinds in the video-editor ('clip' / 'group-child-boundary' / 'group-edge') into ONE unified ClipEdgeResizeSession with a single applyClipEdgeMove function that dispatches on context internally. This is the missing structural item from the previous cleanup megaplan — that one extracted shared math functions but left the session lifecycle (setup → update → end → commit) triplicated, and the triplication is the cause of 'I can't even tell which path the user's gesture went through' during debugging.

State BEFORE this megaplan:

DONE:
- One ResizeSession discriminated union with three kinds: 'clip', 'group-child-boundary', 'group-edge'
- Shared pure math functions in src/tools/video-editor/lib/resize-math.ts: computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, computeGroupEdgeResize
- cursorOffsetPx captured at pointer-down for all three kinds
- Single ref-backed preview store via useSyncExternalStore
- useTimelineScale hook centralizing pixel↔time
- 12 end-to-end resize integration tests in TimelineCanvas.test.tsx covering off-center cursor on interior boundary and group-edge drags
- 222/222 video-editor tests passing
- tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for video-editor
- A temporary [resize-diag] console.log in the 'group-child-boundary' branch of updateResize (needs removing)

NOT DONE (this megaplan):
- The session lifecycle is still TRIPLICATED: three branches each in handleResizePointerDown, updateResize, endResize
- 'group-edge' uses a special startGroupEdgeResize closure pattern that captures commitResize at session start
- useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd has its own internal branching for 'interior vs free' via getInteriorNeighbor

Target shape:

ONE session type: ClipEdgeResizeSession with fields { pointerId, rowId, clipId, edge: 'left'|'right', cursorOffsetPx, initialBoundaryTime, context: ClipEdgeResizeContext }. The discriminator moves from session to context sub-object.

ClipEdgeResizeContext =
 | { kind: 'free'; initialStart; initialEnd; minStart?; maxEnd? }
 | { kind: 'interior'; pairStart; pairEnd; draggedClipId; adjacentClipId; draggedInitialStart; draggedInitialEnd; adjacentInitialStart; adjacentInitialEnd }
 | { kind: 'outer'; shotId; trackId; groupInitialStart; groupInitialEnd; groupClipIds; groupChildrenSnapshot: Array<{clipId;start;end}> }

ONE core function in lib/resize-math.ts:
applyClipEdgeMove(context, edge, newBoundaryTime) -> { updates: Array<{clipId, start, end}>, wasClamped: boolean }
Dispatches internally on context.kind. 'free' returns one update, 'interior' calls computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, 'outer' calls computeGroupEdgeResize and projects children.

ONE updateResize: computes newBoundaryTime from clientX - cursorOffsetPx, calls applyClipEdgeMove, writes updates to override store. Zero branches on kind.

ONE endResize: one branch on cancelled, no branch on session kind. Calls applyClipEdgeMove one more time and invokes onClipEdgeResizeEnd with updates.

ONE commit path in useClipResize.ts: onClipEdgeResizeEnd takes { session, updates } and builds one applyEdit. startGroupEdgeResize closure is REMOVED.

ONE handleResizePointerDown: uses new resolveClipEdgeResizeContext helper to resolve context up-front based on rows/shotGroups/positionedShotGroups/clipId/rowId/edge, then constructs the unified session.

Hard requirements:

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised grouped-drag plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still leaves `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` stale on cross-track moves. the data model stores `trackid`, and current drag/move helpers only update rows/meta/clip order, so a pinned group dragged to another track would keep pointing at the old track unless the drag commit also patches group metadata. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 2.4 against `materializedata()` in `usetimelinecommit` and `assembletimelinedata()` in `timeline-data.ts`. the plan still says to overwrite `pinnedshotgroups` after `materializedata()` returns, but `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so mutating the config afterward would leave signature-derived state stale unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the grouped-drag plan against `useclipdrag` and `usetimelinetrackmanagement.movecliptorow()`. the plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still does not say how `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` is updated when a grouped drag or move lands on a different row, so the overlay and menu state can still point at the old track after a successful cross-track move. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the cross-track multi-drag new-track branch in `useclipdrag`. that path still commits through `applyedit({ type: 'config', resolvedconfig })` using `buildconfigfromdragresult()`, and the revised plan does not explain how pinned-group metadata is updated atomically on that path, so grouped drags that create a new track are still under-specified even beyond the stale-`trackid` problem. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the proposed shared interaction-state location against the actual hook graph in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:194`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinestate.ts:172-204`, and `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinestate.contexts.ts:72-166`. rev 3 says the ref should live in `usetimelinestate.contexts.ts`, but that file only memoizes context values while `useclipdrag`, `timelinecanvas`, and `usepinnedgroupsync` are all coordinated from `timelineeditor`, so the stated location and rationale are still technically off.         Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Collapse the three parallel resize session kinds in the video-editor ('clip' / 'group-child-boundary' / 'group-edge') into ONE unified ClipEdgeResizeSession with a single applyClipEdgeMove function that dispatches on context internally. This is the missing structural item from the previous cleanup megaplan — that one extracted shared math functions but left the session lifecycle (setup → update → end → commit) triplicated, and the triplication is the cause of 'I can't even tell which path the user's gesture went through' during debugging.

State BEFORE this megaplan:

DONE:
- One ResizeSession discriminated union with three kinds: 'clip', 'group-child-boundary', 'group-edge'
- Shared pure math functions in src/tools/video-editor/lib/resize-math.ts: computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, computeGroupEdgeResize
- cursorOffsetPx captured at pointer-down for all three kinds
- Single ref-backed preview store via useSyncExternalStore
- useTimelineScale hook centralizing pixel↔time
- 12 end-to-end resize integration tests in TimelineCanvas.test.tsx covering off-center cursor on interior boundary and group-edge drags
- 222/222 video-editor tests passing
- tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for video-editor
- A temporary [resize-diag] console.log in the 'group-child-boundary' branch of updateResize (needs removing)

NOT DONE (this megaplan):
- The session lifecycle is still TRIPLICATED: three branches each in handleResizePointerDown, updateResize, endResize
- 'group-edge' uses a special startGroupEdgeResize closure pattern that captures commitResize at session start
- useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd has its own internal branching for 'interior vs free' via getInteriorNeighbor

Target shape:

ONE session type: ClipEdgeResizeSession with fields { pointerId, rowId, clipId, edge: 'left'|'right', cursorOffsetPx, initialBoundaryTime, context: ClipEdgeResizeContext }. The discriminator moves from session to context sub-object.

ClipEdgeResizeContext =
 | { kind: 'free'; initialStart; initialEnd; minStart?; maxEnd? }
 | { kind: 'interior'; pairStart; pairEnd; draggedClipId; adjacentClipId; draggedInitialStart; draggedInitialEnd; adjacentInitialStart; adjacentInitialEnd }
 | { kind: 'outer'; shotId; trackId; groupInitialStart; groupInitialEnd; groupClipIds; groupChildrenSnapshot: Array<{clipId;start;end}> }

ONE core function in lib/resize-math.ts:
applyClipEdgeMove(context, edge, newBoundaryTime) -> { updates: Array<{clipId, start, end}>, wasClamped: boolean }
Dispatches internally on context.kind. 'free' returns one update, 'interior' calls computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, 'outer' calls computeGroupEdgeResize and projects children.

ONE updateResize: computes newBoundaryTime from clientX - cursorOffsetPx, calls applyClipEdgeMove, writes updates to override store. Zero branches on kind.

ONE endResize: one branch on cancelled, no branch on session kind. Calls applyClipEdgeMove one more time and invokes onClipEdgeResizeEnd with updates.

ONE commit path in useClipResize.ts: onClipEdgeResizeEnd takes { session, updates } and builds one applyEdit. startGroupEdgeResize closure is REMOVED.

ONE handleResizePointerDown: uses new resolveClipEdgeResizeContext helper to resolve context up-front based on rows/shotGroups/positionedShotGroups/clipId/rowId/edge, then constructs the unified session.

Hard requirements:
1. All 222 existing video-editor tests pass (some may be rewritten to test by context)
2. The 12 end-to-end resize integration tests survive unchanged
3. Add at least one new end-to-end test for free-clip resize (the 'free' context)
4. tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit clean for src/tools/video-editor/
5. The three session kinds are physically gone: grep for kind: 'group-child-boundary' and kind: 'group-edge' in resize lifecycle code returns zero. Three update branches in updateResize and three end branches in endResize are gone. startGroupEdgeResize closure removed.
6. applyClipEdgeMove exists in lib/resize-math.ts with unit tests covering all three contexts
7. The [resize-diag] console.log is removed
8. No regressions in: free clip resize, interior boundary drag, outer edge drag, body drag inside pinned group (useClipDrag - should be untouched), cross-track grouped drag, switch to/from final video, auto-save deferral, migration load, cursor offset tracking

Constraints:
- Scope: resize lifecycle only
- Touch: TimelineCanvas.tsx, useClipResize.ts, lib/resize-math.ts, lib/resize-math.test.ts, TimelineCanvas.test.tsx, possibly TimelineEditorContext.tsx/useTimelineState.types.ts (if startGroupEdgeResize exposed)
- Do NOT touch useClipDrag.ts, data shape, save/sync/persistence, projection migration
- Use existing computeGroupChildBoundaryResize, computeGroupEdgeResize, computeClipResizeMetaPatch inside the new dispatcher — don't reimplement math
- Prefer Edit over Write. Follow CLAUDE.md
- Leave pre-existing non-video-editor test/tsc issues alone

Success criteria:
1. 'boundary doesn't track cursor during drag' symptom cannot reproduce in unified path
2. tsc clean for src/tools/video-editor/
3. All video-editor tests pass — >=223 after adding free-clip test
4. Grep for kind: 'group-child-boundary' and kind: 'group-edge' returns zero in resize code
5. Grep for startGroupEdgeResize returns zero
6. [resize-diag] console.log gone
7. applyClipEdgeMove and resolveClipEdgeResizeContext exist with unit tests
8. Resize lifecycle code line count DECREASES net

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] agent-chat-rendering: removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-chat-undo-integration: agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] agent-tool-utils: agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately. (flagged 2 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised grouped-drag plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still leaves `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` stale on cross-track moves. the data model stores `trackid`, and current drag/move helpers only update rows/meta/clip order, so a pinned group dragged to another track would keep pointing at the old track unless the drag commit also patches group metadata. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 2.4 against `materializedata()` in `usetimelinecommit` and `assembletimelinedata()` in `timeline-data.ts`. the plan still says to overwrite `pinnedshotgroups` after `materializedata()` returns, but `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so mutating the config afterward would leave signature-derived state stale unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the grouped-drag plan against `useclipdrag` and `usetimelinetrackmanagement.movecliptorow()`. the plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still does not say how `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` is updated when a grouped drag or move lands on a different row, so the overlay and menu state can still point at the old track after a successful cross-track move. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the cross-track multi-drag new-track branch in `useclipdrag`. that path still commits through `applyedit({ type: 'config', resolvedconfig })` using `buildconfigfromdragresult()`, and the revised plan does not explain how pinned-group metadata is updated atomically on that path, so grouped drags that create a new track are still under-specified even beyond the stale-`trackid` problem.
